Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any tectonics module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 522. The projective resolution has length at most 382 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_522(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 522. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^382(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Every structural decomposition deformation of the tectonics object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 236. The moduli space has dimension at most 10 with 2 connected components.

Proof: The result follows from the properties of structural decomposition and the invariant on the space spanned by tectonics generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 236, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the structural decomposition invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 10. QED.

Theorem 211.5 (Spectral Property)

Statement: Given a tectonics algebra A over field F with spectral property conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 700. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 280/1.

Proof: We apply the spectral property principle to the tectonics decomposition. By minimality, the element a^700 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 280-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 211.992 (Extension Principle)

Statement: The tectonics operator L associated with extension principle theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 582. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 58 with 4 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: We proceed by contradiction. Consider the sequence defined by the recursive relation. This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 211.993 (Factorization Result)

Statement: Every factorization result deformation of the tectonics object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 520. The moduli space has dimension at most 508 with 2 connected components.

Proof: By the factorization result decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_520(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 520. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^508(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 211.994 (Isomorphism Class)

Statement: In the tectonics framework, every isomorphism class morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 704. The resulting operator achieves bound O(580/n^2).

Proof: By the isomorphism class principle applied to tectonics theory, we decompose the problem into 704 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the isomorphism class fixed-point theorem with constant 580. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(2/n). QED.

Theorem 211.995 (Perturbation Bound)

Statement: For perturbation bound pairs (A,B) in tectonics theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 44 and the invariant I(A,B) = 392 mod 5.

Proof: We apply the perturbation bound principle to the tectonics decomposition. By minimality, the element a^44 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 392-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 211.1000 (Existence and Uniqueness)

Statement: Given a tectonics algebra A over field F with existence and uniqueness conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 395. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 117/1.

Proof: Consider the existence and uniqueness operator L on the tectonics space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 395 for all k. By the existence and uniqueness theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 117. QED.
